# OpenAI — AI lab (private)

_Reference page · generated 2026-06-19 · **Private company — no public financials** (no ticker, no SEC filing, no BBG consensus). Sources: internal corpus (equity-call transcripts, briefings, research library) + web + press + sell-side research, attributed inline. Master index: [../INDEX.md](../INDEX.md)._

## Snapshot
OpenAI is the frontier AI lab behind the **GPT** and **o-series** models, **ChatGPT** (the consumer/enterprise app), and a developer **API**. It sits at the top of the model layer: it consumes enormous amounts of compute (GPUs/accelerators + power), trains frontier models, and monetizes them through (i) ChatGPT subscriptions (consumer Plus/Pro + enterprise/Team), (ii) API/platform usage, and (iii) large one-off licensing/infrastructure deals with enterprises and governments. Flagship model is **GPT-5.5** ("Spud"), released 2026-04-23, positioned as the frontier model for complex professional/agentic work ([OpenAI, "Introducing GPT-5.5"](https://openai.com/index/introducing-gpt-5-5/); [Wikipedia: GPT-5.5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GPT-5.5)). ChatGPT reported **230M+ weekly users** mid-June 2026 (@OpenAI, per `2026-06-19-company-specific.md`); sell-side tracks a broader WAU base of **~920M** as of early 2026, short of the 1B year-end-2025 goal but with a path to 2.75B by 2030 (Barclays Framework, Mar-2026). It is the most prominent — but no longer the dominant-by-share — player in a market it created, now contested by Google Gemini and Anthropic Claude. Barclays judges OpenAI **~6-12 months ahead of competitors** on user growth, monetization and model capability (Barclays OpenAI, Nov-2025).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) ChatGPT subscriptions (consumer Plus/Pro + enterprise/Team, ~50%+ of revenue) · 2) Developer API / platform usage (~17%) · 3) Enterprise agents + large licensing/infra deals (~27%) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Consumers (**>1bn weekly users** — CFO Friar, GS Communacopia 2026-09-08, relayed notes; Altman "just hit a billion", ~late Aug; prior ~920M sell-side mark in Changelog), enterprises/developers, governments; Microsoft as both ~26.8% backer and Copilot distribution channel |
| **Key suppliers** | Compute: Oracle OCI (~$300bn) + Microsoft Azure (~$250bn) of ~$650bn obligations; NVIDIA GPUs (≥10GW), AMD (6GW), Broadcom custom ASICs (10GW), AWS Trainium; Stargate (SoftBank/Oracle) |

## Position in the value chain
OpenAI is the **model/application layer** sitting between compute suppliers and end demand. Historically Microsoft Azure was the exclusive compute backbone; OpenAI has since **diversified** — adding AWS, NVIDIA, custom silicon with Broadcom, and Stargate (SoftBank/Oracle) capacity — while Microsoft remains the largest single backer (~26.8% economic interest) and a primary distribution channel via Copilot ([Alpha Spread](https://www.alphaspread.com/market-news/mergers-acquisitions/openai-restructures-as-public-benefit-corporation-microsoft-takes-27-stake)).

_Microsoft relationship: largest backer (~26.8% economic interest, ~17.6x on ~$13bn invested at the $852bn mark) and a major distribution surface via Copilot; the Azure compute exclusivity has been relaxed as OpenAI diversifies to AWS, custom Broadcom silicon, and Stargate. Pre-PBC sell-side framing put Microsoft's stake higher (UBS estimated **~35%+ equity**, MSFT owning OpenAI IP rights **through 2030** and a reported **~20% revenue share** — UBS OpenAI deep-dive, Sep-2025). MS estimated OpenAI explained **49% of CY25 and 100%+ of CY26 Azure AI growth**, with MSFT's equity-method losses capped at its total investment (MS MSFT/OpenAI, May-2025). ([Alpha Spread](https://www.alphaspread.com/market-news/mergers-acquisitions/openai-restructures-as-public-benefit-corporation-microsoft-takes-27-stake))_

**🆕🔴 2026-09-03 — OPENAI'S SHARE OF [[AVGO]]'s AI REVENUE NEARLY DOUBLES FY27→FY28 (13% → 24%), AND BROADCOM IS DESIGNING MORE THAN THE CHIP.**
_(Broadcom post-print callbacks, 2026-09-03: JPM, UBS · Nathalia Winkler, MS · Joe Moore. ⚠️ **OpenAI as described by its silicon supplier — no OpenAI disclosure in this window.**)_
- 🔴 **THE NUMBERS: JPM's callback splits AVGO AI revenue as **FY27 — [[ANTHROPIC]] 49%, [[GOOG]] internal 29%, OPENAI 13%, [[META]] 10%; FY28 — Anthropic 48%, OPENAI 24%, Google internal 19%, Meta 10%.** ➤ **OpenAI is the fastest-growing line in Broadcom's customer book and becomes its second-largest AI customer by FY28, displacing Google.** **UBS independently: *"OpenAI is projected to reach approximately **5 gigawatts in Fiscal Year 2028**, potentially making it the **second-largest customer**, with another Frontier lab becoming the largest."*** ⚠️ **MS's readout of the same event attributes a 5GW figure to ANTHROPIC next year instead — see [[ANTHROPIC]] for the basis conflict. Both can be true; do not merge them.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL POINT THAT MATTERS MOST FOR OPENAI'S COST CURVE — BROADCOM IS DOING THE RACK, NOT JUST THE SILICON.** UBS: *"For [[GOOG]] and [[META]], who possess established hardware teams, Broadcom primarily supplies the chips. **However, for Frontier labs like OpenAI, which may lack the same internal know-how, Broadcom is 'far more engaged in developing the RACK ARCHITECTURE,' working with partners like Celestica for rack assembly.** This deeper engagement implies **higher value-add and potentially greater content per gigawatt** for these customers."* ➤ **JPM's content split confirms the price of that dependency: OpenAI sits in the **$13-15bn/GW** Broadcom-content bucket versus **$11-12bn** for Google/Anthropic, *"as these customers leverage more of Broadcom's networking capabilities."* **OpenAI is paying a higher Broadcom content per gigawatt than the labs that design their own racks — that is a measurable cost of not having an internal hardware team.**
- **ROADMAP MARKS FROM THE PRIMARY AVGO CALL, RE-CONFIRMED IN THE CALLBACKS: OpenAI **1.3GW of Jalapeño in 2027 · >5GW of Jalapeño + successor in 2028**; the next OpenAI XPU is *"approaching tape-out."* BofA's allocation math uses **OpenAI 1.3GW** as an FY27 input.** UBS adds a definitional caveat worth keeping: *"'XPU' when mentioned in the context of Frontier labs **may encompass future generations of TPU**, not necessarily a completely distinct program."* ➤ ⚠️ **That materially blurs the "OpenAI custom chip" story — part of what is booked as OpenAI XPU may be Google-architecture TPU silicon deployed on-prem.**
- ⚠️ **THE CUDA-MOAT ANGLE, FROM A COMMENTATOR NOT A BROKER: *"model-designed chips erode the competitive moat of CUDA (**OpenAI used its own models to help design Jalapeno**)"* (Vital Knowledge · Adam Crisafulli, 2026-09-03).** ➤ **Logged as a characterisation; it is the clearest articulation yet of why [[NVDA]] bought Hugging Face the same morning.**
- 🔴 **AND THE COUNTERPARTY QUESTION IS NOW THE SELL-SIDE'S LEADING BEAR ON BROADCOM: MS names *"financing concerns and IPO clarity"* around the frontier labs as the main negative on an otherwise bullish AVGO note.** ➤ **OpenAI's funding cadence is now a direct input to a listed supply chain's revenue quality — track it as such.**
- **SAME-WINDOW TRADE PRESS (The Information, 2026-09-02/03 — ⚠️ *reported, not confirmed*): *"New Reasoning Strategies Sweep Through OpenAI, Other Developers"*; *"[[ANTHROPIC]] Splits From Google, OpenAI Over State AI Safety Bill."*** No numbers adopted.
**🆕🔴 2026-09-03 (PRIMARY — THE JPM CALLBACK TRANSCRIPT ITSELF, WHICH UPGRADES THE RELAY DIRECTLY ABOVE) — THE SHARE LADDER IS CONFIRMED FROM THE SOURCE, "JALAPEÑO" IS SPOKEN ON A BROKER CALL, AND JPM ANSWERS THE ONE STRATEGIC QUESTION THE RELAY DID NOT: COULD OPENAI GO CUSTOMER-OWNED-TOOLING AND CUT [[AVGO]] OUT?** *(JPM "AVGO post-FQ3 group callback" · Harlan Sur, 2026-09-03; Speaker 2 = Harlan Sur (JPM semis), Speaker 1 = "Josh" (JPM). Held the day after [[AVGO]]'s FQ3 FY26 print, and carries Hock Tan clarifications given to JPM ON the callback.)* ⚠️ *OpenAI as characterised by its silicon supplier's analyst — no OpenAI disclosure, and OpenAI is private so no rating, PT or estimate exists.* [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_09_03_jpm_avgo_call.html)
- 🔴 **THE SHARE LADDER, FROM THE PRIMARY: OpenAI is **13% of Broadcom's AI revenue in 2027, rising to 24% in 2028** — one of **four >10% customers** next year ([[ANTHROPIC]] 49%, [[GOOG]] internal 29%, OPENAI 13%, [[META]] 10%), becoming **the SECOND-LARGEST behind Anthropic by 2028** (Anthropic 48%, OPENAI 24%, Google internal 19%, Meta 10%).** ➤ **This is the customer-diversification story stated as Broadcom's own frustration: *"there's all this debate about Google internal and [[MEDIATEK]] and [COT] and all this nonsense, when the market has wanted customer diversification — the market's getting customer diversification."* OpenAI is the fastest-rising line in that book.**
- **THE PROGRAMME NAME IS SPOKEN ON THE CALL: the OpenAI ASIC with Broadcom is named **"Jalapeño"** (the ASR transcript renders it *"Halapeno"*).** ✅ **Corroborated — not an ASR garble: the same codename is already on this page from the UBS Hot Chips download and the Barclays read-out of 2026-08-31, and from the [[AVGO]] primary of 2026-09-02. Three independent sources, one name.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE STRATEGIC QUESTION THE RELAYS DID NOT ASK — COULD OPENAI GO **COT (customer-owned tooling)** AND DESIGN THE WHOLE THING ITSELF, THE WAY GOOGLE IS NOW DOING FOR ITS TRAINING CHIP? JPM'S ANSWER IS NO, AND THE REASON IS R&D SCALE: *"can [[OPENAI]] do this? Can [[ANTHROPIC]] do this? **The answer is, I don't think so. It's going to take them a long time** if they try to bring in [COT]... **unless you can amass the type of massive R&D scale that Google has**... it's going to take a significant amount of time for anybody to amass that large of a team where they can even think about doing their own [COT]-like work. **So is it possible? Yeah, it's possible. Is it going to happen anytime soon? The answer is NO.**"* And the sequencing is explicit: *"the NEXT step for Anthropic is to do their own ASIC, **like [[OPENAI]] is doing [Jalapeño] with Broadcom**."*** ⚠️ **TERMINOLOGY GUARD: the transcript renders customer-owned tooling as **"CLT"** and once as **"customer on tooling"**. It is **COT**.** ➤ **The read for this page: OpenAI's silicon independence is bounded by design-organisation scale, not by willingness. The custom-ASIC route with a merchant partner IS the frontier-lab path for the visible horizon — which makes the Broadcom relationship stickier than the "labs will in-source everything" bear case assumes, and makes the [[GOOG]] COT migration a Google-specific event rather than an industry template.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT GUARD, CORRECTED AT SOURCE — AND IT RETIRES A CHARACTERISATION THIS PAGE HAS BEEN CARRYING. OpenAI sits at the **HIGH end of Broadcom CONTENT per gigawatt, $13-15bn/GW** (with [[META]], SoftBank/[[ARM]], ByteDance and Alibaba), against **$11-12bn for [[GOOG]] and [[ANTHROPIC]]**, *"because those customers do take advantage of quite a bit of Broadcom networking capabilities"* while Google's specialised cluster design *"uses LESS Broadcom networking and more of Google's own proprietary networking"*. JPM's own back-of-envelope average is **$13bn**. **BUT: Hock's $20-30bn/GW figure is NOT Broadcom content** — on the callback he *"did clarify that that's **not a Broadcom content per gigawatt**, that is more of a, call it, **hardware infrastructure** [investment] required on a per gigawatt basis."*** ➤ **This page (and the [[ANTHROPIC]] page) previously described the $20-30bn/GW as *"AVGO chip content"*. That reading is RETIRED — see Changelog. Four bases now circulate from this one event: Broadcom CONTENT $11-15bn/GW · total HARDWARE INFRASTRUCTURE $20-30bn/GW · Anthropic ARR $30bn/GW · fully-loaded COST ~$5bn/GW/yr. Never net, chain or average them.**

## State of play
**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-14/15 — THE ENTERPRISE-TRUST CLAIM MEETS ITS FIRST NAMED COMMERCIAL CONSEQUENCE: MARQUEE ACCOUNTS ARE RESTRICTING, AIR-GAPPING OR ROUTING AROUND THE MOST ADVANCED MODELS OVER DATA-RETENTION AND IP FEARS, AND THE NAVIER-STOKES DENIAL IS NAMED AS ONE OF THE TWO TRIGGERS.** (**The Information**, 2026-09-14, headline *"Palantir, Nvidia and Booz Allen Hamilton are restricting Anthropic's Fable model for sensitive work over concerns about its data-retention policies"*, **556k views on @theinformation** — reaching this page via **Rothschild & Co · Rowan Adley**, *"TMT Daily"*, 2026-09-15. ⚠️⚠️ **TMT SPECIALIST SALES SUMMARISING TRADE PRESS — NOT RESEARCH, NOT AN OPENAI DISCLOSURE. No rating, no price target, no model; nothing sized.**)
- 🔴🔴 **THE CLAIM, VERBATIM:** *"**Enterprise IP fears push marquee customers to restrict, air-gap or route around the frontier models.** A widening set of large enterprises — **Palantir, Nvidia, Booz Allen Hamilton, a US utility and Northrop Grumman** among them — are curbing use of **Anthropic's and OpenAI's** most advanced models over **data-retention and IP concerns**."* ⚠️⚠️ **READ THE ASYMMETRY BEFORE READING THE HEADLINE: the story is reported on [[ANTHROPIC]], whose named June policy for its flagship — *"the right to retain rolling 30-day customer usage logs to defend against misuse"* — is the origin, and the per-company behaviours named (the Nemotron substitution, the cyber-code bar, the abandoned utility pilot, the air-gapped open-source stack, Palantir's refusal to distribute) are ALL described against Anthropic's model, NOT OpenAI's. OpenAI is swept into the class, and its named trigger is a DENIAL, not a policy: *"OpenAI denying claims it trained on a user's data for its Navier-Stokes breakthrough."* Do not read this as five accounts restricting OpenAI. Read it as the segment repricing custody, with OpenAI exposed through an allegation it has denied.**
- 🔴 **AND THAT ALLEGATION IS ALREADY A LIVE, UNRESOLVED ITEM ON THIS PAGE — THIS IS ITS FIRST SECOND-ORDER COST.** The page carries the Navier-Stokes training-exposure story from 09-09, and carries OpenAI's field-level rebuttal from 09-10 (*"when you're using a ChatGPT business account, that is your data. We have a ZERO DATA RETENTION POLICY"*), delivered one day after the story broke — **and, in the same BTG session, an attendee put the training-exposure question directly to the OpenAI rep, who declined to answer it** (*"I choose not to wade into it too deeply"*). ➤ **A denial plus a declined question is exactly the shape that produces the behaviour now being reported. The company's own 08-19 positioning was the strongest possible version of the promise — ZERO DATA RETENTION retained for frontier models plus *"Private Safety Processing"*, *"designed to improve safety without giving OpenAI personnel access to the underlying content"* — and WSJ framed it competitively at the time. That framing has now inverted: the same custody question is being used against both labs.**
- ⚖️ **THE LABS' ANSWER, CARRIED IN FULL:** *"Both labs stress they don't train on enterprise data by default and only collect de-identified metadata, and **both now offer self-storage of safety logs (Anthropic's rolls out this autumn, though it reserves the right to withdraw the option)**."* ➤ **The parenthetical revocability caveat is attached to Anthropic; no equivalent caveat is reported for OpenAI's self-storage offer, and none should be assumed either way. The competitive question for this page is narrow and testable: does OpenAI offer IRREVOCABLE zero data retention where Anthropic will not? [[PLTR]] has published the exact condition — it *"won't offer [the rival flagship] through its software until Anthropic grants NON-REVOCABLE ZDR"* — so the bar is now written down by a third party and either lab can clear it.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE COMPETITIVE ANGLE THAT MATTERS MOST TO THIS PAGE IS NOT ANTHROPIC — IT IS [[MSFT]]:** *"**Microsoft and Salesforce push their own isolated environments as a privacy buffer.**"* Restated bluntly on the tape by **@Hesamation (FinTwit, 2026-09-14 13:20, 475 likes / 44k views):** *"even Nvidia and Palantir don't trust OpenAI and Anthropic with their sensitive data… **and Microsoft, which owns 25% of OpenAI, is using these fears to lure OpenAI customers to its own AI solutions.**"* ⚠️⚠️ **THE 25% IS THE TWEET'S CLAIM AND IS NOT A VERIFIED STAKE — logged as the tweet's words only and NOT adopted as a cap-table fact.** ➤ **Strip the unverified number and the mechanism still stands, and it is the sharpest version of the investor-versus-competitor tension this page already tracks: Microsoft is simultaneously an INVESTOR, a COMPUTE COUNTERPARTY and a CHANNEL, and an isolated-environment pitch monetises distrust of the model provider it part-owns. Hold it against the 09-11 Microsoft IR/management fireside already logged here, and note that this is a claim about Microsoft's SALES MOTION, sourced to FinTwit and a specialist-sales relay of trade press — not to Microsoft.**
- ⚠️ **THREE SAME-DAY THE INFORMATION HEADLINES ARE LOGGED AS DATED SIGNALS ONLY — BODIES NOT RETRIEVED, CONTENTS NOT INFERRED:** *"Why Anthropic's Data Policy Drama Is Good for OpenAI"* (2026-09-15 15:31), *"Developers Find Ways to Use Claude Code Without Anthropic Models"* (2026-09-15 15:47), *"Anthropic, OpenAI 'Pacing' Spooks Some Startup Customers"* (2026-09-15 20:26). ➤ **The first and the third point in opposite directions for this page — a rival's custody problem framed as OpenAI's gain, and the pacing debate framed as a shared customer-confidence cost. Neither is evidence; both are dated markers to chase with the primary.**

**[2026-09-14 INGEST - SOURCE DATE UNCONFIRMED] GREG BROCKMAN'S "AGI ERA" / DEFENDER-WINDOW INTERVIEW.** *(User-pasted Gemini transcript of the a16z Show, titled "Greg Brockman Says AGI Has Arrived"; source date, episode page and independent transcript provenance are not stated in the pasted material. The claims below are preserved as **Brockman commentary**, not independently verified OpenAI disclosures.)*
- **THE STRATEGIC CLAIM IS COMPUTER USE, NOT A SINGLE BENCHMARK:** Brockman says Astra is a step-function model because it can operate through screen pixels, keyboard and mouse, allowing agents to use ordinary software without bespoke APIs/MCP connectors; he says OpenAI has seen it run coherently for **24 hours** on long-lived tasks and considers this a reasonable point to call the **"AGI era,"** while acknowledging a still-jagged capability profile. The read-through is agentic adoption and task-based inference demand, not proof that AGI has been objectively achieved.
- **THE "DEFENDER WINDOW" IS A TWO-SIDED CYBER THESIS:** Brockman describes the Hugging Face incident as both a monitoring/sandboxing wake-up call and a preview of what broadly diffused cyber-capable models could do. He says OpenAI redirected **25% of production engineers** to use its models to find and fix security issues, and is building a "defense factory" covering vulnerability discovery, triage, remediation, deployment and validation at machine speed. These are statements in the pasted interview, not a separately verified company release; cross-filed to [AI x Cybersecurity](themes/ai-cybersecurity.md).
- **CAPITAL ACCESS IS PART OF THE SAFETY RESPONSE:** Brockman says OpenAI has made a **$1bn commitment** to help hospitals, water providers, governments and other frontline/critical-infrastructure defenders access models, with discounted access alongside CrowdStrike. This is a claimed commitment, not a public contract or revenue figure in the supplied material.
- **COMPUTE REMAINS A DISTRIBUTION BOTTLENECK:** he expects model capability to continue advancing faster than affordable access can scale, but argues that safety, security and alignment standards may become the binding "pacing the frontier" constraint. He also links data-center acceptance to domestic policy, citing closed-loop water at Abilene and community/college benefits; no independently verified power, water or job figures are adopted here.
- **EXECUTION FOCUS:** Brockman says OpenAI cancelled or deprioritized "side quests" such as Sora to focus on a unified consumer/enterprise ChatGPT experience, Codex and the business basics, after spending the prior two years on data centers, infrastructure and ML engineering. This reinforces the page's existing platform/consolidation thesis; it does not create a new financial forecast.



**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-11 — THE CREDIT FILE: OPENAI IS 97% OF NVIDIA'S GUARANTEE CAP, AND THE PORTS-PIKE CONTRACT NOW CARRIES AN EXPLICIT INVESTMENT-GRADE TRIGGER** (SemiAnalysis (Daniel Nishball, Oliver Kennon, Terence Ong) "Nvidia's Backstop Universe – Heads I Win, Tails Who Loses?", 2026-09-11 — third-party credit work on [[NVDA]]'s 10-Q, not an OpenAI disclosure)
- 🔴🔴 **CONCENTRATION IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, NOT NVDA'S BALANCE SHEET:** *"OpenAI is 97% of the guarantee cap, and the agencies are worried about how much of it points at one customer."* NVDA *"can spread the next tranche across other labs and across Neoclouds, but it cannot diversify away the fact that **two frontier labs are the end payers behind almost every floor, lease and guarantee. That means that a demand shock at the labs hits everything, all at once.**"* ➜ Practical consequence for OpenAI: the vendor-credit channel that financed Ohio is close to its concentration ceiling — *"if possible, the next tranche should go to new counterparties"* (SemiAnalysis, 2026-09-11).
- 🔴 **THE RATING BECOMES A CONTRACTUAL EVENT:** NVDA's exposure *"declines as OpenAI pays and terminates altogether if OpenAI reaches a satisfactory credit rating"*; and on the un-exercised **3.8GW second phase**, *"if Nvidia declines to backstop this phase, then OpenAI has to find an alternative guarantor with an A- credit rating or better."* The $105B cap covers ~**4.25GW on 20-year leases to an OpenAI affiliate**, land/power/shell only, phasing in as buildings reach ready-for-service with **the first expected in fiscal 2029**; OpenAI **pays NVDA for the guarantee (terms undisclosed)** and takes **SB Energy warrants** through the broader relationship (SemiAnalysis, 2026-09-11). ⚠️ **$/GW here = obligation intensity, not revenue/watt and not build cost.**
- 🔴 **THE MICROSOFT LEG, FROM THE COUNTERPARTY: the economics run one way and outlast the IPO** — *"we don't pay them a rev share… they still do pay us a rev share"*, no model rents *"given we own the IP"*, and **all OpenAI IP free to Microsoft "until the end of 2032"**; exclusivity on stateless/stateful is gone (*"free to go source compute and have their API served wherever it makes sense for them"*) but Microsoft **remains primary cloud provider and all OpenAI solutions "need to ship first on Azure"**; last disclosed stake **27%**, equity method (Microsoft IR/management fireside hosted by Jefferies (London), 2026-09-11 — MANAGEMENT source, not Jefferies research).

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-07 — THE ASTRA PRICE CARD IS A BARBELL, NOT A CUT, AND THAT REVERSES THE READ THIS WIKI MADE 24 HOURS EARLIER.** (Jefferies · **Thomas Chong / Zoey Zong**, "AI Series #69: AI Productivity; Model Tiering and Pareto Frontier", **2026-09-07**)
- 🔴 **"In terms of pricing, GPT-6 API PRICE IS 2.5x OF GPT-5.6 SOL. Separately, OAI LOWERED PRICES FOR GPT-5.6 LUNA AND TERRA BY ABOUT 80% AND 20% RESPECTIVELY, mainly due to (1) models are more efficient to run and [OAI] pass[es] on the gains to customers with lower prices for Luna (fastest and affordable) and Terra (balanced model for everyday work); (2) For Luna / Terra, the models provide a cost-effective way to handle HIGH VOLUME WORK at high levels of quality. That makes Meta's unchanged Muse Spark pricing LESS anomalous than the 09-06 entry implied, and it makes the frontier-price direction the OPPOSITE of what a "too cheap to meter" narrative would predict.**
- **Demand-side evidence in the same note: the SILICON DATA LLM TOKEN EXPENDITURE INDEX "hits BELOW 1.0 and reaches BOTTOM week of 30 AUG… It reflects APPEAL OF COST-EFFICIENT MODELS", and OpenRouter tokens grew only +1.8% w/w (vs +21% the week before).** ➤ *A token-EXPENDITURE index falling while token VOLUME grows is exactly what the barbell predicts: buyers migrating down the ladder. Watch whether the 2.5x flagship price shows up as a re-acceleration in the index or as further mix-down.*
